市場焦點
美國
公債回購
• 美國財政部9月9日宣布，將於翌日回購最高60億美元的10至20年期美債，低於部分市場期待
的80億至100億美元規模 。民間持有美債規模達 31.8兆美元，相較之下 ，本次60億美元回購
僅約占民間持債規模 0.02%，對整體影響有限 。此外，約6成的美債期限不足 5年，意味未來
再融資需求仍高，若財政赤字未明顯收斂，長端殖利率上行壓力仍存。
日本央行
• 日本第二季GDP年化季增率上修至 1.4%(優於初值1.1%)，儘管伊朗戰爭引發原油供應緊張給
經濟帶來壓力，但GDP增速依舊高於0.7%潛在經濟成長率，雖然經濟表現支持日本央行(BOJ)
逐步實施貨幣政策正常化 ，但更關鍵的催化劑來自日美兩國協調干預匯市 ，遏制日圓極端貶
值的政治壓力，彭博經濟研究預期日本央行將在9月會議上加息25個基點。

美國
通膨
• 美國8月生產者物價指數 （PPI）年增5.4%，略高市場預期的 5.3%，核心PPI年增4.6%，符合
預期。8月消費者物價指數（CPI）年增3.35%、核心CPI年增2.4%，均符合市場預期，但核心
CPI月升幅0.3%，高於市場預期的 0.2%。數據公佈後，FedWatch顯示市場預期9月升息機率，
從前一周的50%攀升至86%。

美國8月通膨數據(CPI)
• 美國8月通膨數據：8月美國CPI月增0.4%、年增3.4%，核心CPI月增0.3%，核心服務通膨升溫至0.5%，顯示通膨降溫仍不
均衡，能源價格上漲為主要推手，市場預估8月核心PCE月增約0.3%、年增約3.8%，仍高於Fed 2%目標，搭配強勁就業數
據，9月升息1碼的壓力升高。不過，本次通膨主要來自能源、需求及AI投資帶動，與2021～2022年供給型通膨有所不同。
• 市場反應與展望：CPI公布後，2年期美債殖利率一度上升，惟長天期殖利率反而走低，顯示市場關注焦點並非單純升息，而
是Fed能否維持抗通膨政策可信度。整體而言，升息未必等同Risk-off，若經濟與企業獲利維持韌性，股市仍具支撐。
資料來源： investing.com，2026.9.14

燃料成本料推動8月運輸成本上升

能源成本回升推升PPI，通膨壓力牽動Fed政策空間
• 能源成本回升，PPI重新升溫：美國8月PPI月增0.4%、年增5.4%，增幅均明顯高於7月，主要受到商品價格上漲帶動；其
中最終需求商品價格月增1.1%，能源價格上升4.2%，柴油更是大漲逾2成，成為商品價格上升的重要來源，惟最終需求服
務價格僅略增0.1%，顯示本次PPI升溫仍以能源及商品價格為主。
• 成本壓力仍在，利率政策不確定性升高： 扣除食品、能源及貿易服務後的核心 PPI月增0.3%，微低於7月的0.4%，但年增
4.7%，顯示價格壓力仍未完全消退；其中運輸與倉儲服務價格上升2.3%，反映部分成本仍具黏著性，致使市場對Fed利率
政策的重新定價明顯升溫，進而推動股、債市短線波動擴大。
• 市場觀察：由於本次PPI升溫主要來自能源成本 ，但服務價格壓力整體仍相對溫和 ，因此尚不足以視為通膨全面升溫 ，然

能源價格若持續走高 ，將提高Fed維持較高利率的壓力 ，建議投資人短線宜降低長天期債券與高估值成長股的追價 ，債券
可優先配置短中天期、高品質債券，股票則可關注能源、金融及具定價能力企業，提高投組對利率與成本波動的承受度。
資料來源：U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics，Producer Price Indexes – Aug,  2026，2026.9.11
季調後PPI最終需求價格指數月變動

• 財政部對美債的回購規模不如預期：美國財政部9月9日宣布，將於翌日回購最高60億美元的10至20年期美債，主要鎖定流

動性較低的舊券，以改善長天期債券市場流動性。雖然本次回購高於先前設定的每次至少40億美元，但低於部分市場期待的
80億至100億美元規模。消息公布後10年期公債殖利率逼近5%，30年期殖利率亦突破5.3%。
• 回購規模在高額赤字背景下，難以有效拉低長天期公債殖利率：美國今年公債發行規模較2025年增加11.8%，民間持有美債
規模達31.8兆美元、年增8.2%，相較之下，本次60億美元回購僅約占民間持債規模0.02%，對整體影響有限。此外，約6成
的美債期限不足5年，意味未來再融資需求仍高，若財政赤字未明顯收斂，長端殖利率上行壓力仍存。反觀新興國家基本面
持續改善，且當地債市有貨幣潛在升值空間，可作為全球債券配置的分散來源。

地緣政治動盪推升油價，但全球經濟依然展現韌性
市場共識未來12 個月預期每股盈餘
(以2025 年1月為基期=100)
資料來源：Datastream，高盛證券，2026.8.10資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.8.31
全球主要市場及國家經濟成長率
新興市場
美股
日股
歐股
• 地緣政治動盪推升油價，但全球經濟依然展現韌性：美國和伊朗的衝突已經進入第7個月，能源價格上升及地緣政治風險仍
對全球經濟與市場信心形成壓力，但AI投資熱潮持續推升科技需求，使全球經濟展現優於預期的韌性。其中，亞洲仍是全球
成長動能最強的區域之一，2026年亞洲（不含日本）經濟成長率預估達4.7%，明顯高於全球平均2.9%及成熟市場1.6%。在
內需與科技出口支撐下，新興亞洲基本面相對穩健，具備較佳的中長期配置條件。

• AI資本支出延續，台韓成為核心受惠市場：AI資本支出持續擴張，2026年全球雲端AI資本支出預估達7,960億美元，並帶動
AI半導體市場至2030年突破7,500億美元。亞洲掌握全球半導體供應鏈核心，台灣受惠於先進製程與先進封裝需求，韓國則
受惠於HBM記憶體需求，台韓可望成為AI基礎建設擴張的主要受惠市場；同時，中國與印度則提供較穩定的內需及經濟成
長支撐。2026年亞洲企業盈餘成長率預估達72.13%，顯著高於全球平均，建議新興亞洲以台韓科技股為主要成長配置，並
搭配中國、印度市場分散區域風險。

顧
短債供給增加未引發融資緊張，短天期債券防禦價值提升
• 回購市場維持穩定，短債供給暫未造成壓力：美國回購市場今年以來維持平穩，儘管9月短債償付與企業繳稅可能帶來短
暫資金波動，但SOFR與IORB利差仍處相對正常區間 ，顯示目前準備金與短期流動性尚稱充裕 。此外SOFR/IORB差值對
短期美債淨發行增加的敏感度明顯低於2025年底，反映流動性環境偏寬鬆。
• 貨幣基金成長放緩，供給吸收能力受到限制：零售資金流入偏弱，使貨幣市場基金資產增速落後 ，2026年迄今僅增加約
325億美元，遠低於2025年同期的2,050億美元；即便Q4季節性回升，新增資金對短債供給的承接能力仍可能有限。
• 後市觀察：年底前短債淨發行規模預估約3,250億美元，後續需關注短債供給、企業繳稅及財政部長端公債回購對資金面

的影響。建議投資人配置上可提高 1～3年期美國國庫券或短天期美債 ETF比重，以取得票息收益並降低長端殖利率波動
對債券價格的影響。
資料來源：US Treasury，FRBNY ，Federal Reserve ，2026.9.9

風險偏好升溫與債券供給增加，推升期限溢價與長債殖利率
• 全球期限溢酬上升，長債面臨重新定價：全球債券殖利率同步走高，不僅反映各國政策利率預期，也受到全球期限溢酬上升
推動；投資人對持有長天期政府債券要求更高補償 ，顯示長債殖利率上行已具有跨市場的結構性因素 。Bloomberg
Economics的模型亦顯示，全球期限溢酬是近年G7長債殖利率走高的重要共同因素。
• 風險資產吸引資金，放大長債供需壓力：AI投資題材推升股票市場、信用利差仍相對偏窄，加上大宗商品走強，使股票、公
司債及商品與政府債券形成資金競爭；在政府融資需求持續增加的情況下 ，若債券需求未同步擴張，政府公債需提供更高殖
利率才能吸引資金。
• 後市觀察：長端利率已成為股債配置的重要變數 ，若殖利率因財政供給與風險偏好維持高檔 ，長天期債券及高估值股票均可

能承壓；建議以短中天期美債及高品質投資級公司債為核心 ，待長端殖利率充分反映期限溢酬後，再分批拉長債券存續期間。
資料來源：Bloomberg Economics，2026.9.9
全球期限溢價因子推升債券殖利率上行
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.9

日本央行升息箭在弦上，但缺乏連續升息條件
• GDP修正支持升息 ，但關鍵點在配合日本和美國政府遏制日圓疲軟： 日本第二季 GDP年化季增率上修至 1.4%(優於初值
1.1%)，儘管伊朗戰爭引發原油供應緊張給經濟帶來壓力，但GDP增速依舊高於0.7%潛在經濟成長率，雖然經濟表現支持日
本央行(BOJ)逐步實施貨幣政策正常化 ，但更關鍵的催化劑來自日美兩國協調干預匯市 ，遏制日圓極端貶值的政治壓力 ，彭
博經濟研究預期日本央行將在9月會議上加息25個基點。
• 缺乏連續大幅加息的支撐力道： (1)經濟衝擊風險：日圓升值效應已對經濟產生緊縮效果 ，彭博預估可能拖累實際 GDP成長
達0.3%。(2)財政負擔過重：若政策利率升至 1.75%以上，將大幅增加政府利息支出 ，擠壓高市內閣的財政刺激空間 。(3)企

業投資放緩：私人資本支出仍顯疲軟(季比下降0.9%)，顯示經濟復甦基礎尚未完全穩固。
• 小結：日本央行在政治、匯率與經濟基本面交集下 ，9月升息已成定局，但考量財政負擔與全球金融穩定後 ，其後續加息步
伐應偏向謹慎與循序漸進 ，密切關注9月日本央行會議後聲明 ，若央行強調循序漸進而非連續緊縮 ，市場波動相對可控；反
之，若釋放過度鷹派訊號，則需防範市場拋售風險。由於市場資金對日圓套利交易依賴度仍高，日圓任何短期內的急速升值，
都極易觸發全球風險資產的連鎖去槓桿效應。

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.8

中國8月通膨回升，惟需求端疲弱
• 8月份 CPI及PPI：消費者物 價指 數 (CPI)月增 0.4%(前月月減 0.1%)、年增 0.8%(前月 0.5%)，生 產者物價指數 (PPI) 月增
0.4%(前月月減0.7%)、年增3.8%(前月3.5%)。通膨走揚主要受到能源及通訊設備成本上升影響。
• 觀點：中國自2023年以來，內需市場面臨房地產崩盤 、青年失業率飆升以及公共債務攀升等困境 。今年年初以來，通膨雖
然由負轉正，不過從細項得知，因國際油價飆升、AI硬體設備面臨漲價壓力，本波通膨回升主要是受到供給端影響。外部成
本壓力恐進一步壓抑中國內需消費，中國要在真正意義上走出通縮環境可能還需要更多官方政策發力。彭博預估，在需求未
出現明顯改善的情境下，中國央行需要近一步放寬貨幣政策，預測年底前可能降息10個基點。

資料來源：Bloomberg，截至2026.9.9。
受需求低迷影響，彭博預估通膨仍將放緩 8月能源及通訊設備成本上升
資料來源：Bloomberg，截至2026.9.9。

AI產業獲利亮眼，但需留意變現能力和資本效率
• AI獲利增速領先大盤，銷售成長成為獲利關鍵：AI板塊在2026年第一與第二季分別實現52.8%與59.7%的高獲利成長，受高
基期影響，未來四季度平均獲利成長預計放緩至 34.4%，但仍大幅優於非 AI板塊12.3%。預計2026年第三季至2027年第二
季，AI平均銷售成長率將達 27.2%，為非AI成分股(5%)5倍以上 。隨著未來利潤率擴張放緩 (預計未來 6個季度平均僅提升
1.66%，低於過去12個季度的3.52%)，持續的銷售成長是維持獲利的關鍵。
• AI供應鏈內部發展並不均衡： 半導體最為強勁，2026Q3至2027Q2預期銷售成長65.8%，獲利大增112.1%且淨利潤率大幅
擴張8.86%，為產業鏈核心引擎。雲端巨頭表現穩健，營收與獲利增速維持約26%，基礎設施軟體與硬體也有15%~20%營

收與獲利成長 。但應用端軟體成長相對乏力 ，未來四季度獲利預期增速僅 4.3%與6.5%，表現低於標普500非AI股平均水準。
• 資本強度創新高 ，但自由現金流承壓： AI資本支出佔營收比重自 2024年Q1的8.9%一路攀升至 2026年Q2的20.1%，預計
2026年下半年將達到23%的高點。資本支出激增導致自由現金流與資本支出之比(FCF/Capex)從2.48倍滑落至0.69倍，自由
現金流利潤率亦從21%降至13.9%。隨著現金逐漸消耗，企業可能需透過債券市場進行發債融資 ，以維持龐大基礎設施投資。
• 小結與投資建議：AI板塊依然是美股獲利驅動力，但隨利潤率提升空間受限、自由現金流受資本支出壓制，投資邏輯已從全
面暴漲轉向注重變現能力與資本效率。投資建議聚焦上游硬體與半導體，建議持續優選具備高定價能力與實質訂單支撐的半
導體與雲端基礎設施龍頭，留意資本支出佔比過高且自由現金流急劇惡化公司，優先選擇現金流充沛、融資成本低企業。
標普500指數盈利成長：AI vs. 非AI
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.7

標普500指數剔除AI後利潤率或下降
AI股票：自由現金流 vs. 資本支出

歐央9月升息一碼，措辭更鷹經濟預測上修，12月或再升息
• 歐洲央行9月升息一碼：歐央將三大政策利率各上調25個基點，存款利率升至
2.5%，反映能源價格受地緣政治因素推升，同時上調2027年和2028年的核心
通膨預測分別上修了 0.1個百分點。與此同時，對於經濟成長前景更為樂觀 ，
上調了今年的GDP成長預測，主要反映了2026年第二季數據優於預期以及下
半年內需表現更為穩健 ，此外，年末成長動能的增強為 2027年創造了更有利
的延續效應，也推動了明年經濟成長預期的上調。
• 投資建議： 此次會後 聲明基調偏鷹 ，強調通膨衝擊預計還將延續 ，市場預期
12月或再升息 ，這將有助銀行業利差 ，進一步支撐營收與獲利表現 ，同時，
企業投資與融資需求回升，加上製造業景氣改善帶動放款成長，均有助於歐洲

銀行業擴大收益來源， 建議投資人可以適度配置，增加投組多元性。

美國8月通膨數據(CPI)
資料來源： investing.com，2026.9.14
近五年美國8月CPI年增率
燃料成本料推動8月運輸成本上升
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.14
• 美國8月通膨數據：8月美國CPI月增0.4%、年增3.4%，核心CPI月增0.3%，核心服務通膨升溫至0.5%，顯示通膨降溫仍不均衡，
能源價格上漲為主要推手，市場預估8月核心PCE月增約0.3%、年增約3.8%，仍高於Fed 2%目標，搭配強勁就業數據，9月升息
1碼的壓力升高。不過，本次通膨主要來自能源、需求及AI投資帶動，與2021～2022年供給型通膨有所不同。
• 市場反應與展望：CPI公布後，2年期美債殖利率一度上升，惟長天期殖利率反而走低，顯示市場關注焦點並非單純升息，而是

Fed能否維持抗通膨政策可信度。整體而言，升息未必等同Risk-off，若經濟與企業獲利維持韌性，股市仍具支撐。

1. 本資料內容僅供內部同仁及客戶參考，非作為任何金融商品或服務之購買或銷售之推介，不得視為買賣有價證券或其他金融商品之要約或要約之引誘。
2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

### Page 2

For the month of October 2026, we estimate AirTAC’s monthly revenue will decrease 
to Rmb729mn (-10% MoM, +16% YoY)/NT$3,447mn (-10% MoM, +28% YoY) 
We expect October revenue will go down by 10% MoM to Rmb729mn/NT$3,447mn, 
mainly due to 19% fewer working days in Mainland China. However, we expect to see 
improvement in daily pneumatic revenue (Rmb32.3mn in Oct vs. Rmb 29.4mn in Sept) as 
the improvement in daily pneumatic revenue is driven by the strong growth from key 
customers, including electronics, followed by machine tools and battery, and we further 
expect demand to improve following the end of low season.

Earnings and TP revisions 
We revise down our 2026E earnings estimates by 1% to factor in actual 3Q26 revenue 
(revise down 2026 revenue by 1%). We revise down our 2027E/2028E earnings estimate 
by 1%/1% to factor in a more conservative assumption on battery and linear guide 
business (revise down AirTAC revenue by 1%/1% in 2027E/2028E). We continue to see 
the company focusing on cost control as well as experiencing a better product mix 
outlook, but we expect some offset by rising material cost (revise down 2026E/2028E 
OPM by 0.1/0.1ppt).

### Page 3

Maintain our Buy rating on AirTAC, with a new 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925), 
based on an unchanged target P/E of 26x on 2027 EPS.

Investment view: Accelerating monthly YoY from smaller segments could be a surprise. 
Maintain positive view on AirTAC 
We reiterate our Buy rating on AirTAC as we continue to believe the company is a key 
beneficiary of the factory automation/industrial automation localization trend in 
mainland China, and we expect the company will also benefit from the prolonged 
upcycle supported by the ongoing government stimulus policies. We see multiple key 
end-segments’ performance tracking ahead of the company’s 2026 full year guidance of 
20%+ YoY growth (see here), with battery/textile/machine tools/electronics delivering 
55%/46%/41%/23% YTD YoY growth while total revenue in Rmb is tracking at 23% YTD 
YoY growth. 
We continue to see solid monthly YoY growth from smaller segments, such as energy 
lighting and textile. However, the battery segment, which is the key growth driver in 
2025 (with 89% YoY in 2025), is seeing its first negative monthly YoY growth since Oct 
2024, while other major contribution sectors, such as electronics and automotive are 
seeing decelerating monthly YoY growth. As a result, we remain cautious on the 
potential of exaggerated orders (including advance or duplicate orders) despite new 
orders intake continuing to outpace shipments, and we will continue to monitor the 
underlying end-demand closely.

### Page 4

Maintain Buy on AirTAC with a lower 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925). 
Investment Thesis - AirTAC 
AirTAC is the 2nd-largest pneumatic supplier in mainland China with 28% market share 
in 2025, outpacing most local competitors, owing to its low-cost yet high quality 
product, and service quality reputation in most key cities in mainland China. We forecast 
its market share will reach 30% in 2026E and expect AirTAC to be the structural winner 
in a deflationary market, due to its: (1) strong market growth and leadership: AirTAC 
continues to expand its market share in mainland China driven by competitive pricing 
that is more than 10% lower vs. international peers (with 45%+ GM / 30-35% OPM) and 
high demand elasticity among mainland China customers; and (2) superior cost structure 
and financial health: with best-in-class cash cost and a solid balance sheet, featuring 
positive net cash over FCF since 2023/2024, we believe AirTAC is well-equipped to 
weather industry downturns and capitalize on market restructuring. 
Overall, we believe AirTAC will see better revenue/profitability in the long term given its 
share gainer position in the China pneumatic market and potential to gain share in the 
linear guide market (improved demand in the next 1-2 years). This should lead to 
strengthened valuation, vs. its recent trading range (trading below its 10-year avg. P/E 
multiple). We are Buy rated on AirTAC. 
Price target risks and methodology - AirTAC 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$1,895 is based on a target P/E of 26.0x on 
our 2027E EPS (0.25x STDV above avg of the last pneumatic upcycle). 
Key downside risks: (1) Stronger competition from peers; (2) sharper-than-expected 
Rmb depreciation, and (3) a longer-than-expected end-market demand downtrend in 
mainland China.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 12 Oct 2026 close.

their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

OSAT 展望樂觀，SOIC 機台具技術優勢 
 2027 主要成長動能仍為 OSAT:2026 年弘塑產能擴充一倍 ，1Q26 已
經完成擴建 ，3Q26 新產能已經滿載。 外包部分 ， 目前外包比例33%，
未來佔比會下降，目前外包月產能一個月 5-6 台，因為外包產能受
限，同業也在搶外包產能。2027 主要成長動能仍為 OSAT，今年晶
圓代工客戶營收高於 OSAT，2027 年 OSAT 營收超過晶圓代工客戶，
SOIC 機台去年小量，今年量增，2027 跟 2026 差不多略增，2028
才會明顯大增，主要還是跟隨晶圓代工客戶 SOIC 擴廠進度。 
 2028-2029 年 PLP 機台出貨量會很明顯放大:PLP 及 CoPoS 設備機
台單價很高，初估有機會是 CoWoS 翻倍，PLP 進度比較快，PLP
規格弘塑 310*310、510*515、600*600 都有，再大到 700 以上就不
做了，晶圓代工客戶規格是 310*310 但目前進度比較慢，還在實驗
線階段。力成 510*515 進度最快，4Q26 會拉第一批機台，2027 隨
力成購置的友達廠進度持續拉機台，2028-2029 年 PLP 機台出貨量
會很明顯放大。 
 維持買進評等，目標價 3000 元：基本面無變化，預估數字也沒做調
整，故維持買進評等 ，預估 2026 年 EPS 64.81 元，2027 年 EPS 93.97
元，目標價 3000 元。(2027 PER 30x)

 弘塑為本土封測設備龍頭廠商 
弘塑為本土封測設備龍頭廠商，主要利基產品為半導體後段濕製程設備，並延
伸相關化學品、檢測設備代理及軟體解決方案。1H26 產品結構可區分為機台檢
測產品(65%)、化學產品(20%)、代理設備(15%)。2013 年合併化學品公司添鴻
(CLC)、2018 年底合併通路商佳霖(ClC)、併購資訊系統廠太引(Tyne)，投資美
國 X-Ray 檢測公司 SVXR。 客戶為國內半導體 、 光電 、記憶體及面板產業大廠，

懋、中芯、宏達等。

2Q26 毛 利率 39.37%， 低於預
期，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元 
 2Q26 毛利率 39.37%，低於預期，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元 
弘塑 2Q26 EPS 11.18 元，低於元富預估的 12.34 元，獲利達成率 91%。本業
來看，2Q26 營收 17.27 億元，+8%QOQ，+6%YOY，2Q26 毛利率 39.37%，
較 1Q26 回升 5.1 個百分點，然仍低於預估的 41%，主要是外包成本及急單增
加影響部分毛利率，預期 3Q26 毛利率持續向上改善，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元。

弘塑 3Q26 營收 22.67 億元，+31.3%QOQ，+51.8%YOY，營收達成率 98%，
略低於預期，預估 3Q26 毛利率 41%，預估 3Q26 EPS 16.47 元。

### Page 1

ab 12 October 2026
Global Research
Genius Electronic Optical
Core resilience with CPO upside optionality
Raise PT to NT$1,250 on potential CPO contribution and intact core business
We reiterate Buy and raise our  price target to NT$1,250 from NT$700, rolling valuation
to 2028E and moving from P/B to a P/E-based SOTP that separately values the core lens
business and the emerging co-packaged optics (CPO) business. We believe Genius is
making progress sampling three precision components inside the fiber array unit (FAU)

targeting FAU integrators such as Coherent and FOCI.  We see potential for the company
to qualify the leading FAU module supplier into NVIDIA's CPO program. At the same
time, its core business is outperforming Largan, growing +1% YoY in Q326 vs. -11%
YoY for Largan as its share rises on periscope lens. Compared with the pressure on
Largan's margins from green electricity prices, we believe Genius should maintain its
margin, as its main production site is in Xiamen, whereas Largan's production in Taiwan

is affected by higher electricity prices.
Core intact: periscope share gains offset muted smartphone demand
September sales of NT$2.67bn (−2% MoM, +1% YoY) took Q326 to NT$7.82bn (+38%
QoQ, +1% YoY), in-line with VA consensus. Genius grew 10% YoY in 9M26 against 2%
at Largan, which we attribute to share gains in periscope lenses for premium iPhones,
offsetting legacy price cuts and lower VR lens shipments. We still keep 7% below VA on
4Q26E revenue (NT$7.34bn, −6% QoQ) reflecting muted iPhone sales and no content

in the iPhone 18 Pro's new variable-aperture lens.
CPO component strategy offers a different route to market from Largan
Unlike Largan's pursuit of complete FAU assembly, Genius is targeting high precision
optical components within the module, including: 1) high-precision prism to collimate
light from the PIC into the fibers, 2) V-groove/key slot to precisely hold the fibers in place,
and 3) MT ferrules (high density plastic connector bundling and aligning fibers). We

believe Genius' customer is one of the key module suppliers on NVIDIA's 2H27 program
targeting over 50% share. Our base case models a complete-FAU market of US$1.85bn
in 2028E and US$4.45bn in 2030E, with Genius base case for US$185m and US$668m
representing 10% and 15% revenue share of the complete-FAU revenue TAM.
Valuation: Maintain Buy with  PT lifted to NT$1,250
We fine-tune our 2026/2027 basic EPS estimates from NT$37.2/NT$41.5 to NT$36.7/NT

$44.0 and introduce 2028E EPS of NT$49.25. We move from P/B to P/E SOTP as the core
business now delivers steadier earnings and CPO warrants separate valuation. Our NT
$1,250 PT comprises NT$745 base business  (15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) and NT$505
for CPO (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in 2028E). Catalysts include qualifications at
leading FAU makers and the board approved 7mn share private private placement with
potential strategic investor (albeit 6% dilution if issued). Our US$1.8bn CPO value

compares to US$7bn implied in our Largan NT$5,000 PT.

### Page 2

Genius Electronic Optical UBS Research
Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Could Genius' CPO products contribute meaningful revenue in a few years?
Yes, from H227E into 2028E. We see Genius' CPO strategy as feasible and lower-risk than Largan's.
Largan's goal is to supply the complete FAU; Genius is entering at the component level, supplying V-
grooves, MT ferrules and prisms to FAU makers. This lets Genius sell into multiple FAU designs and

integrators, limiting its downside if the market converges on a single winning architecture.
Management believes multi-sourcing is inevitable once volumes scale and has seen early indications
of potentially substantial demand from US optical players, including Corning and Coherent. Genius
has a partnership with FOCI and is pursuing other FAU integrators including we believe Coherent;
management suggested further updates could come in the near term. Some parts already ship in

small volumes, but we do not expect meaningful revenue until H227 or 2028.
Q: Will Genius benefit from further lens technology upgrades in the coming years?
Yes. We expect premium lens participation and product mix to support core revenue growth of 8.0%,
9.7% and 9.1% in 2026-28E. We believe Genius is gaining share from Largan in Apple's periscope
lens supply chain after initially lagging. Apple (70–80% of Genius' sales) sell-through units grew 9%

YoY YTD through August according to Counterpoint, and Genius' 9M26 revenue grew 10% YoY on
a similar trajectory. Largan grew only 2% in 9M26, with Q326 down 11% YoY. For the iPhone 18
series, Genius participates in every lens spec except the variable-aperture upgrade, where it stepped
back because of pricing pressure from Chinese competitors. Management says Genius has the
technical capability and could enter if future specs or design changes improve the opportunity.

Genius offers a combination of an established earnings base from smartphone lens and potential
exposure to the passive components required for optical interconnects. We remain constructive on
the lens business and see CPO as a potentially material addition from 2028. The component strategy
provides several routes to customers, but the valuation should reflect uncertainty over qualified
content, allocation and margins. Our Buy rating and NT$1,250 PT is based  on NT$745 base business

(15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) plus NT$505 for the CPO earnings (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in
2028E). The CPO option for Genius is US$1.8bn vs. US$7bn implied in our Largan NT$5,000 PT.
Genius share gains are showing in reported sales, with YTD sales +10% YoY vs. +2% YoY for Largan.
The company is also participating in every spec except variable aperture.  For CPO, Genius delivered
samples of its prism, V-groove, and MT ferrule to FOCI at the end of May, with initial feedback broadly

on track and no major issues reported to date. Our checks indicate the company is also positioning to
supply one of the leading FAU module suppliers for NVIDIA's CPO program from H227.
At NT$1,060, the shares trade at 24.1x our 2027E core EPS and 21.5x our 2028E core EPS, factoring
some of the CPO opportunity in the multiple rather than in estimates relative to the 10 year average
17x P/E. Applying a 15x valuation to the smartphone lens business + 40x to the CPO option from our

scenario of NT$9.30 EPS in 2028E yields upside from the last close at NT$1,060 to a NT$1,250 share
price.

### Page 4

Genius position in the CPO value chain
Genius is targeting the CPO market as a component supplier while Largan is advancing
from components into full-FAU assembly. Genius is sampling three components for CPO
with FAU makers, including: 1) high-precision prism to redirect light from the PIC into
the fibers, 2) V-groove/key slot to precisely hold the fibers in place, and 3) MT ferrules
(high density plastic connector bundling and aligning fibers). The companies overlap in

capability, with Largan also having PMLA and fiber arrays but not getting into some
components like MT ferrules and V-Grooves.
Genius's optical design, precision tooling, metrology and volume-manufacturing
experience offer it a good entry point for this market. Commercial differentiation will
depend on reproducible optical performance, dimensional tolerances, thermal stability
and customer yield.  It has announced collaboration with FOCI and  we believe has

potential to also work with one of NVIDIA's leading module suppliers for its H227
program.

Annual FAU TAM reaches US$1.85bn in 2028E and US$4.5bn in 2030E in

our base case
We update our FAU (fiber array unit) TAM factoring CPO/NPO penetration into
AI networking switches, NVIDIA multi-rack systems, scale-up on compute and switch
boards, and non-NVIDIA accelerators.  We assume one passive FAU assembly connecting
a fiber array to a photonic engine with one unit per optical engine.
We assume one FAU per optical engine and 32 per switch ASIC in our base case,
matching NVIDIA's 32x3.2T engines on its 102.4T Spectrum-X package. Broadcom's

102.4Tb/s Davisson uses 16 engines at 6.4Tb/s, so our bear case assumes an even mix of
these two designs (24 per ASIC). We note that higher switch bandwidth does not
automatically increase FAU count if engine bandwidth rises at the same rate. Our 2030E
assumption maintains the 32 FAUs per switch based on bandwidth rising to 204.8Tb/s

Our base case for the FAU market sums up 4 opportunities for CPO/NPO attach:
1. Scale-out and scale-across switches
We estimate annual switch ASIC shipments x CPO + NPO penetration x optical engines
per ASIC x FAUs per engine. In 2028E, 25% CPO/NPO penetration or 1.0m 102.4T
switch ASICs at 32 FAUs each produces 8.0m FAUs. In 2030E, 55% of 1.1mn

204.8T switch ASICs at 32 FAUs drives a market size of 19.4mn FAUs.

2. NVIDIA's multi-rack connections for Rubin Ultra and Feynman
NVIDIA at its 2026 GTC San Jose proposed a multi-rack option to network together 8
Oberon NVL72 racks for the Rubin Ultra generation and 8 Kyber NVL144 racks.

Feedback at the time showed  possible transceiver or NPO connections for Rubin Ultra
and CPO connections for Feynman.  Our 2028 base case uses 110,000 racks, 15% multi-
rack adoption and 75% CPO/NPO penetration, giving approximately 12,400 connected
racks.
For 2030E, our rack count falls to 35,000 racks as we assume Kyber would have 4x the
number of GPUs, equivalent to 140k Oberon equivalents. We assume the 35,000 racks
at 55% multi-rack adoption x 80% CPO/NPO attach gives 15,400 connected racks. We

assume about 216 and 576 inter-rack FAUs per rack, respectively, driving a market
opportunity of 2.7mn FAUs in 2028E and 8.9mn in 2030E.

We sum four pools: scale-out/scale-across switches, NVIDIA multi-rack pods, CPO native

adoption and greater content in scale-up systems, despite an assumed FAU ASP decline
from US$150 to US$125.

Scenario analysis on Genius CPO opportunity

### Page 7

We assume in base case Genius captures 10% of the TAM in 2028E and 15% by 2030E.
The base case in 2028E is consistent with US$30 of prism, V-groove and ferrule content
in half of all FAUs or US$15 average content spread across the FAU market.  The
2030E bull case (25%) or US$37.50 would require near full content of some of the sub-
components or otherwise ability to integrate more of the solution value by that time.
Our bear case assumes only 1% share of the opportunity in 2028E and 5% of the

opportunity in 2030E, with EPS even by 2030E falling to NT$4 and yielding limited
valuation potential for the shares.

Reiterate Buy and raise PT to NT$1,250 valuing in CPO optionality

We adjust our valuation method from P/B to P/E SOTP to better reflect the company’s
core business and its opportunity within CPO industry while the core smartphone
business is demonstrating more stable earnings to use P/E vs. prior P/B. Our PT at NT
$1,250 is based on NT$745 base business  (15x 2028E EPS of NT$49.25) plus NT$505
for the CPO earnings (40x base case NT$12.72 EPS in 2028E) with catalysts from
upcoming qualifications into leading FAU makers and potential strategic private

placement with board approving 7mn share private placement (albeit 6% dilution if
issued). We note Genius CPO option valued at US$1.8bn vs. US$7bn implied in our
Largan valuation.
Our price target uses 2028 earnings for both businesses, reflecting the modeled start of
meaningful CPO contribution. We apply 15x core EPS of NT$49.25 and 40x CPO EPS of
NT$12.72 for a NT$1,250 price target. The 40x multiple assumes the optical business
can sustain strong growth after its initial ramp; qualification, concentration and margin

risk limit the confidence that can be placed on that earnings stream.
In the upside case, stronger premium lens participation and manufacturing execution lift
2028E core EPS to NT$54.32. Faster optical adoption expands the 2028E FAU market to
US$3.46bn; 15% effective revenue capture and a 31% pretax margin produce
approximately NT$36.88 CPO EPS. Applying 15x to core earnings and 42x to CPO
earnings gives approximately NT$2,365.
In the downside case, weaker lens demand and margin pressure lower core EPS to NT

$44.29. Slower optical adoption leaves a US$0.46bn FAU market, of which Genius
captures 1%, producing only NT$0.29 CPO EPS. Applying 12x and 38x gives
approximately NT$540. The downside is driven by both a weaker core business and a
much smaller optical contribution.

Upside/Downside Spectrum
Upside/Downside Spectrum
Genius (3406.TW) trading at 1,060 at closed Oct 8.
Source: UBS estimates
UPSIDE (NT$2,365): Lens upgrades accelerate and Genius gains share in higher-end
specs. 2027E/28E sales grow 11.8%/9.1% YoY (base 9.7%/9.1%), and GM expands to
35.6%/35.5% (base 34.4%/34.3%) on better yields and mix. 2028E core EPS of NT
$54.3 (+10% vs base) at 15x gives NT$815. Faster CPO adoption and stronger AI capex

lift the 2028E FAU TAM to US$3.5bn (19.8mn units at US$175); at 15% capture, CPO
sales reach US$519mn and CPO EPS NT$36.9, worth NT$1,550 at 42x.
BASE (NT$1,250):  Core NT$745 (15x NT$49.25) plus CPO NT$505 (40x NT$12.72), as
above.
DOWNSIDE (NT540): Smartphone demand weakens, legacy price erosion continues
and share gains stall. 2027E sales grow 7.4% YoY with GM of 33.4%, and 2028E core
EPS falls to NT$44.3 (−10% vs base), worth NT$530 at 12x. CPO adoption is slow

(10%/3% CPO/NPO in scale-out, no ASIC attach), leaving a US$0.46bn TAM; at 1%
capture, CPO is worth NT$11.

Forecast returns
Forecast price appreciation 17.9%

Forecast stock return 19.5%
Market return assumption 6.3%
Forecast excess return 13.2%
Company Description
Founded in 1989, Genius Electronic Optical (GSEO) is a leading global manufacturer of
camera lenses, providing full service optical solutions, including mobile lenses, automotive
lenses and AR/VR optics. Its production sites are in Taichung (one plant) and Xiamen (two

plants). The Taichung plant mainly manufactures VR lenses, while the Xiamen plants produce
compact lenses for consumer electronics. Optical lens products accounted for 73% of 2023
total revenue, and others include TOF lenses, flash lenses, VR lenses and LED lighting
products. Its major client is Apple and it supplies lenses for iPhones and iPads.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's

interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of
shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our Genius price target is based on PE-based sum-of-the-parts model. Genius' growth is
highly leveraged to the overall handset industry and could be affected by volatility in handset
demand, as well as whether handset OEMs will continue upgrading their camera design.

Genius' margins could be affected by increasing competition and the rise of new
technologies, ASP pressure from customers and fluctuating material and labour costs.
Downside risks include competitors catching up in technology, yield rate issues for new
products and uncertain iPhone demand.
Our price target is based on SOTP valuation on the CPO opportunity and traditional lens
business. We think Largan's downside risks include: 1) ongoing pricing pressure from

smartphone OEM clients and increasingly intense competition among lens suppliers; and 2) a
slower-thanexpected smartphone demand recovery in China, and 3) CPO commercial success
below the market's elevated expectations. We believe the key upside risk is a faster-than-
expected CPO qualification process at Largan. In addition, if the company demonstrates
exceptionally efficient and highly accurate automation capabilities, it could support greater
market share gains within the industry than we currently anticipate.

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

營收走勢圖

研究員/丁宥霖 yulin.ting@uni-psg.com 
       廖婉婷 TINA_LIAO@uni-psg.com

臻鼎 9 月營收 263.58 億元 ， 改寫單月新高，3Q26 營收 646.24 
億元，優於研究部原估的 607.09 億元，主因 AI 相關業務出貨
優於預期 ， 加上手機客戶進入新品旺季 ， 研究部據此上修 3Q26 稅
後 EPS 至 3.84 元（原估 3.47 元）。展望 4Q26，行動通訊旺
季延續， 加上 GPU 與 ASIC 新一代主板開始放量 ，營收與獲利將
續創新高 。2027 年伺服器與光模塊隨新平台出貨擴大及 1.6T 成
為主流， 營收可望各再增逾百億元；IC 載板亦受惠供給持續吃緊
及新產能陸續開出 ， 帶動 AI 相關營收占比突破三成 。 綜上所述 ，
研究部上修臻鼎 2026、2027 年稅後 EPS 至 12.11 元（+99.6% 
YoY，原估 11.81 元）及 26.14 元（+118.6% YoY，原估 22.91 
元）。基於臻鼎產品橫跨軟板、類載板、HDI、HLC 與 IC 載板，
可為客戶提供一站式解決方案 ， 並提前掌握關鍵材料與設備產能 ，
在高階 PCB 供給吃緊下擴產得以大量開出搶奪市占，研究部維
持以 2027 年獲利為評價基礎及 28 倍本益比，目標價上調至 
730 元，維持買進投資評等。

重點摘要： 
1. 9 月營收改寫新高，上修 2H26 獲利預估 
臻鼎 9 月營收 263.58 億元（+27.5% MoM、+36.1% YoY），改寫
單月新高 ， 其中伺服器 、 光模塊與 IC 載板合計營收年增逾三倍 ，
行動通訊亦呈雙位數月增 與年增 。3Q26 營收 646.24 億元
（+33.4% QoQ、+36.4% YoY），達研究部原估的 106.4%，明顯優
於預期 ， 主因 GPU 新一代主板進入量產 、1.6T 光模塊出貨放大 ，
加上手機客戶進入新品旺季。考量 AI 相關產品占比提升，研究
部預估 3Q26 毛利率為 25.5%（+2.4ppt QoQ、+3.5ppt YoY，原
估 24.7%），稅後 EPS 上修至 3.84 元（+81.9% QoQ 、+81.9% 
YoY，原估 3.47 元） 。展望 4Q26，行動通訊旺季延續，加上 GPU 
與 ASIC 新一代主板放量，研究部預估 4Q26 營收 736.13 億元
（+13.9% QoQ、+29.4% YoY），稅後 EPS 4.75 元（+23.8% QoQ、
+70.4% YoY，原估 4.86 元），營收與獲利續創新高。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. AI 伺服器主板成為最主要成長動能 
AI 伺服器主板隨 GPU 與 ASIC 平台升級，晶片封裝尺寸、I/O 
數與傳輸速率同步提升，主板須在有限面積內完成更多高速訊號
的扇出與繞線。 傳統 HLC 以一次壓合搭配機械鑽孔與背鑽 ，層間
連接密度已近極限；高階 HDI 則以多次壓合與雷射盲孔逐層堆
疊，線路更細、訊號路徑更短，可降低高速傳輸損耗，帶動 GPU 
平台 OAM 等主板設計由 HLC 轉向 HDI。HDI 須投入大量雷射鑽
孔機與多次壓合產能，在設備與材料交期拉長下，全球高階  AI 
PCB 產能缺口約 15–20%， 訂單已排至 2028 年，2027–2028 年
產品報價亦將持續上調。臻鼎提早取得全球逾五成高階雷射鑽孔
機產能 ， 在建 22 座新廠中半數投入 HDI、 伺服器與光模塊產品 ，
在同業擴產受限下得以持續承接訂單，帶動伺服器 HDI 比重由 
1H26 的約五成於 2H26 明顯提升；繼 ASIC 專案今年大量出貨
後，GPU 平台新一代主板亦自 4Q26 起放量。研究部預估臻鼎伺
服器營收 2026、2027 年各增加逾 100 億元 ， 為未來兩年最主要
成長動能。

3. 臻鼎可望躍居 1.6T 光模塊 PCB 龍頭 
AI 伺服器傳輸速率由 800G 升級至 1.6T，單通道速率倍增使線
路須更細 、 阻抗控制更精準 ， 傳統減成法已難以達到 ， 光模塊 PCB 
因此改採 mSAP 製程。臻鼎過去為手機類載板主要供應商，具備 
mSAP 大量生產經驗 ； 在高階設備交期拉長 、 關鍵材料供給吃緊下 ，
臻鼎已掌握市場上多數高階設備產能並持續擴充，同時提前鎖定
上游材料，得以在淮安與泰國同步擴充光模塊產能，研究部推估
臻鼎可望在 2027 年 1.6T 世代成為全球光模塊 PCB 龍頭。隨 
1.6T 出貨占比由 2026 年的約五成提升至 2027 年逾七成，研
究部預估臻鼎光模塊營收由 2025 年約 9 億元躍升至 2026 年
逾 100 億元，2027 年再增至逾 250 億元。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

4. ABF 與 BT 同步吃緊，新產能與轉單推升載板營收 
AI 晶片封裝面積與層數持續放大，ABF 載板用量與製程難度同
步提升，惟新廠建置期長、上游材料吃緊，供需缺口持續擴大；
同業亦將部分 BT 產能轉往 ABF，使 BT 載板同步出現缺貨，兩
者報價自下半年起持續上漲。臻鼎 ABF 載板技術規格已達最大 
158×158mm、 最高 28 層 ， 深圳一廠 1Q26 轉盈並將於年底滿載 ，
二廠 1Q27 開始貢獻，第二園區 2028 年起約每年開出一座新
廠，且新產能幾乎全由國際客戶預先包下，高雄廠亦已量產先進
封裝相關載板。BT 載板雖無新建廠計畫， 但近期承接同業轉單並
調漲報價 ， 加上產品與客戶組合持續優化 ， 可望維持雙位數成長 。
隨 ABF 產能接續開出，研究部預估 ABF 占載板營收比重將由 
2025 年的 35% 提升至 2027 年約七成，IC 載板營收 2026、
2027 年分別成長 99.2% 及 89.0%。

5. 高階新產能陸續開出，產品組合優化推升毛利率 
臻鼎 2026 年資本支出逾 800 億元，2027 年維持相當水準，廠
房數將由目前的 28 座擴增至 2030 年底的 59 座。新產能自 
2027 年起陸續開出：淮安 6 座新廠於 2026 年底完工後進入裝
機，HD 園區預計 3M27 投產、2Q27 大批量產；泰國新廠於 4Q26 
起試產，1H27 進入小量生產 ； 深圳第三園區與載板第二園區則預
計自 2028 年起接棒 （表二） 。新廠初期稼動率與良率仍在爬坡，
加上 2027 年折舊費用預計較 2026 年再增加 20% 至 30%，短
期將稀釋毛利率；惟新增產能以 AI 伺服器、光模塊與 IC 載板
為主，隨 AI 相關營收占比由 2025 年的 11.7% 提升至 2027 
年突破三成，研究部預估臻鼎毛利率將由 2026 年的 24.5% 提
升至 2027 年的 29.3%。

6. 上修 2026、2027 年獲利預估 
2026 年 AI 相關業務為主要成長動能 ，研究部預估伺服器 、光模
塊與其他營收成長 269.0%，IC 載板成長 99.2%，行動通訊受惠
摺疊機新品成長 6.9%，電腦消費則與 2025 年持平，AI 相關營
收占比將由 2025 年的 11.7% 提升至 25.9%。研究部預估 2026 
年營收 2,273.96 億元（+24.6% YoY，原估 2,267.70 億元），
毛利率由 24.1% 上修至 24.5%（+4.7ppt YoY ），稅後 EPS 由 
11.81 元上修至 12.11 元（+99.6% YoY）。 
展望 2027 年，伺服器與光模塊隨新平台出貨擴大及 1.6T 成為
主流，營收合計成長逾一倍，IC 載板受惠 AI 客戶訂單放量、
ABF 比重提升及報價調漲，成長 89.0%，行動通訊與電腦消費則
分別維持中、低個位數成長。在高毛利產品占比提升、費用率隨
營收規模擴大回落，抵銷新廠折舊增加下，研究部預估 2027 年
營收 3,158.99 億元（+38.9% YoY，原估 3,083.20 億元），毛
利率提升至 29.3%（+4.8ppt YoY，原估 28.1%） ，營益率提升至 
17.9%（原估 14.8%） ， 稅後 EPS 上修至 26.14 元（+118.6% YoY，
原估 22.91 元）。

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
여전히 가격 상승 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 95.8조원, 영업이익 75.1조원으로 예상한다. 
이전 전망 대비 감소한 수준이다. 원/달러 환율 영향이다. 영업이익률은 78.4%로 전 
분기 대비 2.1%p 상승할 전망이다. DRAM 매출액은 73.1조원, 영업이익 
59.2조원으로 추정한다. DRAM ASP는 이전 전망 대비 상향 조정했다. 일부 제품 
가격 인상을 반영한 영향이다. Bit Growth는 이전 전망 수준을 유지했다. ASP 
상향이 수익성 개선에 미친 영향은 크지 않을 전망이다. NAND 매출액은 22.4조원, 
영업이익은 15.9조원으로 예상한다. 
Global Top 수준 주주환원 규모/자사주 살 게 더 있다 
SK하이닉스는 2026년 8월 19일 주주환원에 대한 새로운 정책을 제시했다. FCF에서 
CAPEX를 제외한 금액의 50% 이상을 주주환원에 사용한다는게 핵심이다. 2026년 
기준으로 최소 90조원 수준일 것으로 추정한다. 자사주 중심으로 주주환원을 계획 
중이고 이미 집행 중인 40조원 이외에 4분기 중에 추가로 자사주 매입이 진행될 
것으로 예상한다. 배당도 일부 집행이 되겠지만 자사주가 중심이 될 전망이다. 
흔들리지 말아야 할 이유는 많다 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 모델의 성장으로 메모리에 
대한 수요가 지속적으로 높아지고 있고, 이에 비해 공급 증가는 제한적인 상황이 
상당기간 지속될 것으로 전망하기 때문이다. 이로 인해 현재 수준의 DRAM, NAND 
가격에 변동성은 낮고, 높은 수익성이 유지되면서 안정적 현금흐름 창출이 가능할 
것으로 전망한다. 현재 주가는 지나치게 저평가되어 있는 국면에 있다고 판단한다. 
목표주가는 12개월 예상 EPS 446,092원에 PER 9 배를 적용한 4,000,000원을 
유지한다.

Document 219: AI“跌倒”，美债“吃饱”！全球“资本争夺战”，市场给出答案

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI“跌倒”，美债“吃饱”！全球“资本争夺战”，市场给出答案.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-24
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

AI 巨头天量资本开⽀引发市场信任危机，纳斯达克创近年最差两周表现，⼀篮⼦ AI 相关股
票单周重挫 4.9% 。与此同时，美债强势逆转， 10 年期收益率跌破 5% ，债券 ETF 单⽉吸⾦
580 亿美元。 AI 扩张与政府⾚字融资同时争夺有限资本，廉价资本时代逻辑正被改写。
摘要：
⼀场围绕资本定价权的争夺正在全球市场上演。 AI 超级资本开⽀周期与各国政府财政⾚字周期同
时向债券市场争夺资⾦，在触发 AI 板块剧烈震荡的同时，将美债推⼊久违的避险买盘。这⼀切指
向同⼀个结论：廉价资本时代的逻辑正在被改写。
本周，纳斯达克 100 指数录得去年 4 ⽉以来最差两周表现，⼀篮⼦ AI 相关股票单周重挫 4.9% ，
Meta 等科技巨头遭遇重创。
与此同时，美债市场强势逆转， 10 年期美债收益率时隔⼀个⽉⾸度跌破 5% ，⻓端债券 ETF 连续
三周上涨，债券资⾦流⼊规模本⽉已达约 580 亿美元，占全部 ETF 流⼊的 38% 。
资本的⽤脚投票，揭⽰出市场情绪的深层转变。此前近乎⽆懈可击的华尔街共识 —— 做多⼤型

科技股、做空防御性资产正遭受越来越多的质疑。 Neuberger Berman 多资产联席⾸席投资官
Jeffrey Blazek 直⾔：
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

AI 开⽀叙事动摇，市场信任出现裂痕
过去两年， AI 交易⼏乎是华尔街最坚不可摧的共识：做多⼤型科技股，做空防御性资产。这⼀共
识本周遭遇实质性挑战。
投资者的焦虑集中于⼀个核⼼问题：科技巨头为 AI 投⼊的天量资本，能否在⾜够短的时间内产⽣
⾜以匹配的回报？
华尔街⻅闻提及，Anthropic 未来数年可能在额外算⼒上耗资 7500 亿美元。 Meta ⾸席执⾏官扎克
伯格随后宣布，该公司将在未来算⼒及 AI 基础设施租约与合同上⽀出约 8250 亿美元。
消息公布后， Meta 股价本周⼤跌逾 9% ，为 5 ⽉以来最⼤单周跌幅，公司信⽤⻛险指标同步升⾄
历史⾼位。其他超⼤规模云计算商及芯⽚制造商的债务保护成本也随之跳升。
与此同时，全球企业债信⽤利差普遍⾛阔。衡量美国企业债市场感知⻛险的关键指标， CDX 投
资级指数周五盘中最⾼升⾄ 64.4 ，为 3 ⽉以来最⾼盘中⽔平。该指数越⾼，表明投资者对信⽤⻛
险的担忧越深。
值得注意的是， AI 相关股票组合与 10 年期美债收益率已连续九周同向波动，这是⾃ OpenAI 推出

ChatGPT 以来持续时间最⻓的联动⾛势。
这是彻彻底底的剧情反转 —— 我们正在看到资⾦争相涌⼊国债，与 AI 交易形成背离。

### Page 3

这与 7 ⽉的情形截然不同 —— 彼时 AI 交易降温，美债同样承压。当前格局意味着 AI 信⼼的动摇，
正被市场直接转化为对安全资产的需求。
美债 " 避险 " 属性重归，资⾦防御转向加速
债市的反转来得迅猛。本周初，⼤量投资级债券供给推动 10 年期美债收益率在周⼀升⾄
5.14% ；随后 AI 恐慌情绪袭来，曲线急速反转。
美债周四全线⼤涨 12 ⾄ 18 个基点，以中段表现最为突出，创逾⼀年来最佳单⽇表现， 10 年期收
益率收于 4.97% ，为⼀个⽉来⾸次收盘跌破 5% ，亦是⾃ 6 ⽉以来⾸次跌破 50 ⽇均线。
⾼盛的 Christian Gattellari 表⽰，" 我们现已突破所有 CTA 短期买⼊触发点 " ，趋势追踪者空头仓
位达到最⼤空仓的 87%。野村的 Charlie McElligott 则告知客⼾，债券正重新 " 发挥对冲功能 " 。
NewEdge Wealth ⾸席投资官 Cameron Dawson 对此作出判断：
资⾦流向数据同样印证了这⼀防御转向。据 Baird Strategas ，股票与固收 ETF 需求之间的缺⼝已

跌⾄历史低位⼗分位区间。
彭博数据显⽰，本⽉已是固收连续第⼆个⽉吸引超过 35% 的整体 ETF 流量，这是⾃去年初以来⾸
次出现连续两个⽉的情形。彭博智库分析师 Athanasios Psarofagis 表⽰，
AI 与政府争夺同⼀池资本
市场动荡的背后，有⼀个更为结构性的张⼒正在形成： AI 超级资本开⽀周期与政府财政⾚字融资
周期正在同时挤向同⼀个债券市场。
华尔街⻅闻提及，企业需要建设数据中⼼，政府需要弥补⾚字；私⼈部⻔要融资购买算⼒，公
共部⻔要融资⽀付福利、利息与国防开销。两股融资需求同向暴涨，⽽除⾮储蓄供给同等增
加，资本的价格将别⽆选择，只能上升。
随着此前的股市领涨板块⾛势趋于防御，以及信⽤指数发出压⼒信号，投资者现在有了寻求债券
相对安全性的理由。
过去两个⽉债券资⾦流⼊明显超出常规，部分反映出更具防御性的资产配置倾向，但收益率的吸
引⼒同样促使投资者趁机锁定收益。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
439 收藏 分享到 :
这并⾮危⾔耸听。历史上，铁路⾰命最终改变了美国，但 19 世纪的铁路债务同样引发了多轮⾦
融危机；互联⽹最终重塑了世界，但 2000 年前后的资本泡沫依然破裂。⼀场⻓期正确的产业⾰
命，与中短期资本市场的压⼒，并不互相排斥。
后⾦融危机时代，全球资本市场的运⾏逻辑建⽴在⼀个核⼼假设之上：廉价资本将⻓期存在。
各国政府的财政体系、科技企业的扩张路径、资产价格的估值框架，⽆不依赖这⼀假设。⽽今
这⼀假设正在动摇。
信⽤利差的⾛阔已是可观察的早期信号。若最弱信⽤级别（ CCC 级）的信⽤利差持续承压，意
味着资本紧缩已不再只是利率市场的技术问题，⽽开始向融资能⼒较弱的企业和资产传导。
本周再融资担忧导致  CCC 级债券收益率达到 15.85% ，为 2020 年以来的最⾼⽔平。但⽬前与所

有垃圾债券的收益率差距为 7.48 个百分点，仍⽐ 2016 年和 2020 年达到的先前峰值低 1 个百分
点。
"AI 有价值 " 与 " 任何价格下的 AI 投资都合理 " 之间，存在根本区别； " 政府可以发债 " 与 " 债务可以⽆
限扩张⽽不付出代价 " 之间，同样如此。
当资本被迫在不同需求之间配给，最先承压的是边缘资产和弱信⽤主体，随后蔓延⾄更⼴泛的
资本市场，最终传导⾄就业、消费和实体投资。
债券市场这⼀轮最深刻的变化，或许并不只是收益率升⾄某个具体点位，⽽是市场开始重新接
受⼀个曾被遗忘的事实：钱不是⽆限的。
限免

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -2.62 điểm tương ứng -0.15%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Công nghệ và Máy móc là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -13,987 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã HDB, PNJ và VJC; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-30 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,267 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -2,363 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 3,726 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -2,702 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua một tuần đi ngang giằng co, giảm nhẹ và đóng cửa ở 1.735 điểm 
với thanh khoản ở mức thấp, và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng cả 5 phiên trong tuần. 
Thông tin vĩ mô tích cực với tăng trưởng GDP quý 3 đạt mức gần 10% là điểm sáng trong 
tuần qua, tuy đã không đủ nâng đỡ thị trường chung. Thay vào đó, tâm lý thị trường thận 
trọng trước số liệu lạm phát ở mức cao, đợt tăng giá xăng dầu trong nước và mặt bằng lãi 
suất trong nước chưa có dấu hiệu hạ nhiệt. Diễn biến tăng của giá dầu thế giới, và lợi suất 
trái phiếu Chính phủ Mỹ cũng là các yếu tố gây áp lực lên thị trường chung. Tâm lý thận 
trọng còn thể hiện qua áp lực bán giải chấp ở một số cổ phiếu riêng lẻ như PNJ, SHB..., dù 
một vài nhóm trụ như dầu khí, tiêu dùng có thời điểm đóng vai trò nâng đỡ chỉ số.

Trần Đức Anh 
Giám đốc Kinh tế Vĩ mô & Chiến lược thị trường 
anhtd@kbsec.com.vn 
07/10/2026

Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
Tích lũy trong vùng nhiễu động

Chúng tôi dự báo vùng điểm hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm 2026 
xuống 1,800 đi ểm, giảm mạnh từ mức 1,950 đi ểm đưa ra trong báo cáo gần nhất. 
Vùng điểm này tương ứng:

- Tăng trưởng EPS 2026 so với cùng kỳ của thị trường chung ở mức 33% 
(tăng mạnh so với mức 25% của báo cáo gần nhất), chủ yếu nhờ kỳ vọng 
vào các chính sách thúc đẩy kinh tế của Chính phủ, và lợi nhuận tăng 
trưởng đột biến ở VIC (xem thêm phần Triển vọng hoạt động kinh doanh).

- Mức P/E hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm ở mức 13.2 (giảm 
nhẹ so với mức 13.5 thời điểm hiện tại - phản ánh quan điểm các yếu tố 
rủi ro sẽ tiếp tục duy trì). Đây là mức định giá thấp so với bình quân 5 năm 
gần nhất ở 15.1. Nếu loại bỏ yếu tố đột biến ở nhóm Vingroup, chỉ số P/E 
điều chỉnh hiện đã rơi xuống 10.9, thấp hơn các đáy gần nhất năm 2020, 
2022 và 2025. Trong bối cảnh kinh tế Việt Nam duy trì tốc độ tăng trưởng 
cao, lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm yết liên tục bứt phá, vùng định giá 
này mở ra cơ hội đầu tư trung/dài hạn. Chúng tôi cho rằng ngay khi các rủi 
ro vĩ mô quốc tế (xoay quanh vấn đề Iran), và trong nước (xoay quanh vấn 
đề lãi suất) hạ nhiệt, thị trường chung sẽ có cơ hội tái định giá lên mức cao 
hơn đáng kể so với thời điểm hiện tại. Dù vậy, khác với báo cáo gần nhất 
vào tháng 7, ở báo cáo này, chúng tôi đẩy lui thời điểm các yếu tố rủi ro vĩ 
mô hạ nhiệt sang năm 2027, đồng nghĩa với quan điểm thận trọng hơn về 
thị trường cho quý 4 2026.

Cụ thể, đối với vấn đề lãi suất, áp lực lạm phát lớn dần, đi kèm nhu cầu vốn cao phục 
vụ hoạt động đầu tư cơ sở hạ tầng sẽ khiến mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao cho 
phần còn lại của năm 2026 (dù thanh khoản hệ thống ghi nhận sự cải thiện đáng kể 
trong 1 quý gần đây). Đối với vấn đề xung đột Trung Đông, các cuộc đàm phán vẫn 
đang bế tắc và ít có kh ả năng dòng ch ảy dầu mỏ tại khu vực này được nối lại ngay 
trong năm nay.

Chủ đề đầu tư chúng tôi l ựa chọn cho 4Q2026 là đ ầu tư công, chính sách ti ền tệ hỗ 
trợ, và xung đột Iran.

Xem đầy đủ tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành mẫu nến Doji trên khung đồ thị tuần sau hai tuần giảm điểm liên tiếp, 
cho thấy áp lực bán đã phần nào được kiểm soát. Trên khung ngày, nỗ lực hồi phục đầu 
tuần gặp lực cản tại đường SMA50 khiến VNINDEX quay đầu giảm điểm, tiếp tục chớm phá 
đáy ngắn hạn; tuy nhiên vùng hỗ trợ gần đã tạo hiệu ứng thu hút lực cầu bắt đáy, giúp chỉ 
số thu hẹp đà giảm trong phiên cuối tuần. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh khá tiêu cực từ vùng đỉnh 
đầu tháng 9, VNINDEX đang bước vào giai đoạn tìm kiếm điểm cân bằng trở lại và diễn biến 
giằng co điều chỉnh có thể còn kéo dài thêm trong các phiên kế tiếp. Tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn tính từ vùng đáy cuối tháng 7 vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại điểm đỡ gần quanh 1710 (đây cũng là chốt chặn cần 
giữ để bảo vệ được xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn). 
Kháng cự xa: 1820 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1775 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1710 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1670 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Mã Giá đóng cửa tuần Biến động từ đầu tháng Giá khuyến nghị mua Giá mục tiêu Lợi nhuận  kỳ vọng Điểm nhấn đầu tư Báo cáo phân tích

- Sau khi phủ sóng các thành phố lớn, chuỗi Long Châu đang mở rộng sang các vùng cấp 2, 3. 
Năm 2026 dự kiến mở thêm 400-450 cửa hàng (+20%YoY) với DT trên mỗi cửa hàng cũ tăng 
trưởng 6%-8%/năm

chiếm 50% thị phần doanh thu ngành bán lẻ dược phẩm, từ mức 17%-20% hiện tại

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
SEPTEMBER CPI PREVIEW
Setting the pace for the run into year end
19 October 2026
Key points
• September CPI to rise 0.62%mth 
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr 
from 4.0%yr. The seasonally adjusted 
CPI lifts 0.5%mth, a step down from 
the 0.7% increase in August.  
• September Trimmed Mean (TM) is 
set to lift 0.26%mth holding the 
annual pace steady at 3.6%yr. 
• As we noted in our review of the 
August CPI, our September quarter 
CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr 
while the TM remained 0.9%qtr.  
• Auto fuel surged ahead of our 
expectations so along with signs of 
increase pass-through we lifted our 
December quarter CPI to 1.3%qtr/3.9%yr 
and the TM estimate to 0.9%qtr/3.5%yr. 
• This is a 0.8ppt increase to the CPI 
estimate, and a 0.2ppt increase to the 
TM estimate, highlighting inflation 
momentum meaningfully above 
the RBA’s current expectations.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
August inflation came in soft...
Inflation moderated in August following July’s strong 
outcome. The CPI rose 0.4% in the month, in line with our 
expectation but slightly below market consensus of 0.5%, 
taking the annual rate to 4.0%. On a seasonally adjusted 
basis, the CPI increased 0.7%mth, underscoring the impact of 
seasonal discounting in August and the start of the financial 
year repricing in July.
More closely watched are the measures of underlying inflation 
including the Trimmed Mean (TM) and market services. 
The TM rose 0.2% in August, in line with our expectations 
but again softer than the market, leaving the annual pace 
unchanged at 3.6% for a third consecutive month. Meanwhile, 
market services excluding volatiles & holiday travel increased 
0.2%mth/4.1%yr. Notable strength (three-month annualised, 
seasonally adjusted) was seen in sports participation (10.9%), 
clothing repair (7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other 
household services (5.2%). For full analysis of the August CPI, 
see “August CPI – Came in on our expectations ”.
The expenditure-weighted share of the CPI basket 
experiencing positive price shocks eased to 11% in August 
from 15% in July, while the share experiencing negative price 
shocks declined from 17% to just below 13%. The majority of 
the positive shocks were concentrated in consumer durables 
On a three-month moving average basis, the net momentum 
share (positive minus negative) rose to –4.5% from –7.7% 
in July and a trough of –15.8% in May, suggesting the 
disinflationary impulse may be nearing exhaustion. 
... with September due for a moderate pick-up
Looking ahead to September, early indicators painted a 
mixed picture. The NAB business survey showed retail price 
growth slowing to 0.9%qtr from 1.1%qtr in August, with 
labour cost growth easing to 1.7%qtr from 1.8%qtr, while 
purchase cost growth edged up to 2.4%qtr from 2.3%qtr. 
Nevertheless, all measures remain well above their long-run 
averages while the gap between purchase costs and final 
prices continues to widen, indicating businesses are facing 
margin compression (see here for further detail). Meanwhile, 
consumers’ year-ahead inflation expectations held steady 
at 4.9% in September (easing slightly at 2dp) but surged to 
5.3% in October likely reflecting higher fuel prices, with retail 
petrol and diesel averaging $2.20/l (+8.2%mth) and $2.68/l 
(+7.7%mth). We do believe the auto fuel prices are likely to see 
a near term peak in October.  Meanwhile, global shipping costs 
have also surged recently amid renewed Middle East tensions.
Sep quarter unrevised but Dec upgraded significantly
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% (0.62% to 2dp)
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Our seasonally adjusted
estimate increases 0.5% (0.48% to 2dp). For the monthly
TM, we expect a 0.3% (0.26% to 2dp) lift, holding the annual

pace steady at 3.6%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking
a 1.0% gain in September (noting that while this measure is
conceptually different to the quarterly TM it still serves as a
useful guide). For market services excluding volatiles & holiday
travel we have pencilled in another 0.2% rise, slowing the
annual pace slightly to 4.0% (a nine-month low).
The largest contributors are recreation & culture (+0.27ppt),
driven wholly by holiday travel & accommodation. Transport

(+0.23ppt) makes a solid contribution, with gains in fuel
(+0.26ppt) partly offset by motor vehicles (–0.02ppt). Housing
also contributes (+0.13ppt), with rents and new dwellings
each adding 0.02ppt while property rates add 0.08ppt.
The key detractors in the month are food & non-alcoholic
beverages (–0.08ppt) and garments (–0.04ppt).
Key details for the month of September
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to fall
0.5%mth, easing the annual pace to 2.8%yr. The weakness is

concentrated in a 3.7%mth decline in fruit & vegetables, while
non-alcoholic beverages are also expected to fall 1.5%mth.
Meals out & takeaway is forecast to rise 0.1%mth, easing the
annual rate to 3.9%.
Housing is expected to rise 0.6% in September, slowing the
annual pace to 5.5%yr. Component-wise:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.3%mth and 3.7%yr. CPI rent
inflation is nearing its peak, consistent with the moderation
in asking rent growth. We expect rent inflation to ease to

3.6%yr by end-2026 and 2.5%yr by end-2027.
• New dwelling prices, which capture only the building
component of construction costs, are expected to rise
0.2%mth and 4.9%yr. Ongoing weakness in established
housing markets would be increasing builders’ difficulty in
passing through higher costs to new builds. As a result, we
expect new dwelling inflation to moderate to 4.5%yr by
end-2026 and 2.1%yr by end-2027.
Transport inflation is expected to rise 2.0%mth in September,

lifting the annual pace to 7.7%yr reflecting an 8.3%mth
increase in fuel prices following higher oil prices through the
month. Motor vehicle prices and spare parts are expected to
fall 0.7%mth and 1.3%mth, respectively. In NSW, a one-year
vehicle registration discount between $80-100 came into
effect from 1 September, which is likely to weigh on the other
services in respect of motor vehicles component.
Recreation & culture is expected to rise 2.1%mth driven by a

4.4%mth increase in holiday travel, partly offset by a 0.4%
decline in audio, visual & computing equipment & services and
a 1.3% fall in sporting services.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Risks to September baseline
We view the risks around the September CPI outcome as broadly 
balanced. Waning spending momentum outside of fuel may make 
it difficult for businesses to pass through higher costs in the near 
term. However, some of these pricing pressures may be deferred 
rather than forgone. The October ban on card surcharging may 
prompt a greater-than-usual degree of price resetting, allowing 
businesses to recover some of the margin pressures that have built 
up in recent months.
Risks to housing are mixed. Trade materials suppliers continued 
to announce above-average price increases through September 
(around 8%). While soft conditions in the established housing 
market may limit the extent to which these costs are passed 
through to new dwelling prices, they nevertheless present an 
upside risk to housing-related inflation, particularly maintenance 
& repair and insurance costs. Growth in advertised rents also 
continues to ease but these new leases take some time to flow 
through to average rents paid measured in the CPI. 
A kick up in October ...
We have a solid preliminary estimate for the October CPI of 
0.6%mth lifting the annual pace to 4.8%yr. It is true that the key 
driver is an 8.9% increase in auto fuel but we estimate the TM to 
lift 0.4%mth/3.7%yr highlighting broader inflationary pressures. 
We expect that the removal of credit charge surcharges and 
resulting increase in retail prices, at most, will contribute 0.1ppt 
but only just so see it as more of a rounding error. Nevertheless, 
these changes come at a time of a significant upstream price 
pressure, particularly for SMEs, so there is some risk of broader 
price adjustments. As such, we expect marginally larger than 
usual price adjustments in October particularly in hospitality 
and household services. Being the first month of the quarter the 
October lift sets a higher base for the quarterly average estimates 
resulting in an upgrade to our December quarter estimate.
We have included a table with a monthly profile for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to June 2027. At 
June 2027 we have an annual pace of 3.7%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI and 3.3%yr for the TM. There is also a 
table breaking down the monthly components of the CPI through 
the December quarter. 
... highlights stronger inflationary pressure ...
Our September quarter CPI estimate is unrevised at 1.3%qtr 
with the annual pace steady at 3.9%yr. At the margin there 
was a 0.04ppt increase due to a lift in auto fuel but this is 
1.29%qtr at two decimal places so still rounds to 1.3%. 
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% in the September 
quarter highlighting that part of the increase is due to usual 
seasonality. Our TM estimate for Q3 has a small downward 
revision of 0.03ppt to 0.88% at two decimal place so our 
published estimate is also unrevised at 0.9%qtr. 
... resulting in an upward revision to Q4 2026
Westpac’s December quarter CPI estimate has been boosted 
from a 0.5%qtr to 1.3%qtr. Driving most of this change from 
our previous estimate is the 15.3% increase in autofuel, it

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
was 1.9%, contributing 0.55ppt to the 0.8ppt increase in the 
quarterly estimate. Outside of a 0.09ppt contribution to the

4.2% to 5.6%) all else was via small contributions for a large

A disinflationary impulse kicks in for 2027
We now expect auto fuel prices to peak in October (240 cpl 
for petrol, 284 cpl for diesel) and then to trend lower through 
2027 (184 cpl for petrol and 194 cpl for diesel by December 
2027) leading to a 13.6% falling in auto fuel in the first half 
of 2027 subtracting 0.46ppt off the CPI over the same period 
of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the 
auto fuels profile please see: “ Persistent energy pressures 
have further to run ” 
The first half of 2027 will also see an almost 5% decline in 
holiday travel & accommodation which will subtract a further 
0.29ppt off the CPI. However, outside of that any broader 
moderation in inflation is expected to be more muted hence 
we have the TM printing 0.7%qtr for each quarter in 2027.  
Critical to this moderation in core inflation is our expect step 
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab -
lished home prices. From a 1.1%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we 
see dwelling price increasing by 0.7% in the Q4 2026, and 
then 0.6% in both Q1 and Q2 2027 before dropping to 0.5%qtr 
in Q3 and Q4 2027. This will see dwelling price inflation in the 
CPI drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 3.0%yr by June 2027 
then down to 2.2%yr by December 2027. 
At least for the near term a key uncertainty remains the extent 
to which businesses are able to pass through ongoing labour 
and non-labour cost pressures and how this unwinds through 
2027 as energy prices ease and the domestic economy slows 
under the weight of higher interest rates and falling estab -
lished home prices. There is a risk that firms face greater 
resistance to price increases if discretionary household spend -
ing softens by more than expected. That said, these headwinds 
may be at least partially offset by the stronger starting point 
for household incomes, which has proven more resilient than 
previously anticipated, and by higher energy prices due to the 
ongoing conflict in the Middle East.
That said, there is also the risk that the disinflationary pulse by 
the second half of 2027 could be greater than what we antici-

Document 387: Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Threats to European diesel imports_ risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased risk of price rises (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

Threats to European diesel imports: risk of shortages ruled out for the time being, but increased 
risk of price rises

In Europe, the possibility of a US ban on diesel exports has heightened concerns about price trends for this widely used 
fuel1, which has already risen significantly since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran. It has become a major political issue 
on both sides of the Atlantic in the run-up to decisive elections.

A particularly tight diesel market due to a shortage of buffer stocks 
Following the outbreak of the conflict, diesel prices did indeed see some of the sharpest rises on the oil market due to particularly 
tight market conditions. Since the end of February, whilst the price of a barrel of crude has risen by 46%, diesel prices have soared 
by 100% in Europe and by 76% in the United States.

The factors alleviating pressure on the refined products market, and more specifically the diesel market, were more limited than in 
the case of crude oil. Shipments passing through the Strait of Hormuz (around 17% of maritime diesel trade prior to the conf lict) 
have fallen by around 75%. Added to this is the halt in Russian exports due to war damage, which is depriving the diesel market of 
around 12% of its maritime exports. Furthermore, unlike with crude oil, China has not acted as a stabilising  force in the diesel 
market. On the contrary, the energy crisis has led to a halt in Chinese exports of refined products (including diesel) to the  rest of 
Asia from April onwards. The resumption of Chinese exports over the last two months remains fragile, given, in particular, that 
diesel stocks are at their lowest level in 15 months.

The United States has eased these tensions in the diesel market by increasing its exports of refined products (up 25% on average 
in the first half of 2026 compared with the average over the last five years). This rise in exports has required high refinery utilisation 
rate (close to maximum capacity) and, to a lesser extent, a reduction in stocks (-11% compared with the average over the last five 
years as at mid -September for middle distillates 2). All these factors have contributed to tightening the US domestic market and 
pushed retail diesel prices to record levels. Road diesel prices (US average) have risen by 68% since the end of February and by 
74% year-on-year.

A structurally high level of vulnerability in Europe… 
Against this market backdrop, characterised by mounting tensions and very limited room for manoeuvre , Europe would be 
particularly hard hit by a reduction in – or even a complete halt to – US exports. According to Eurostat, imports accounted for 46% 
of European demand for diesel in the first half of 2026, with 40% of these imports coming from the United States. Europe, which

1 Diesel accounts for 17% of the primary energy mix in the EU and 7.3% in the United States. 
2 A category of refined petroleum products comprising mainly diesel and aviation fuel.

### Page 2

has lost a great deal of production capacity in recent years, is operating its refineries at near -maximum capacity. Its room for 
manoeuvre is therefore limited.

… But the risk of a shortage has been ruled out in the short term 
The risk of a shortage, however, appears limited in the short term. The situation regarding European stocks is not comfortable, but 
it is not alarming. Commercial diesel stocks in the ARA region 3 are currently at historically low levels (around 12 million barrels), 
23% below their level at the end of September 2025. As for emergency stocks (EU Emergency Stocks), these are, according to the 
European Commission, at a sufficient level. Eurostat estim ates this figure at 270 million barrels in June 2026 (stable from a year 
earlier), representing approximately 60 days’ worth of EU consumption. Europe imports around 0.9 mb/d of diesel from the United 
States, and emergency stocks would therefore cover around 60 days’ consumption at 4.5 mb/d.

The rise in prices could be significant 
Whatever the outcome of the conflict, diesel prices are likely to continue to rise in the short term. According to our base -case 
scenario, crude oil prices are expected to remain very high in Q4 2026 due to ongoing tensions in the Persian Gulf, whilst a return 
to normal levels of diesel exports from Russia and the Middle East appears unlikely by that time.

Among the factors likely to cause future tensions, the start of the maintenance season in Europe could affect production leve ls, 
whilst demand for diesel in the United States is typically high in the autumn (agricultural harvests and pre -winter storage). I n a 
scenario where tensions remain low, the diesel refining margin (crack spread) could rise by 5% in Q4 compared with its level at the 
end of September 2026, before gradually falling back during 2027. An intermediate scenario (a 10% rise in the crack spread in Q4 
2026) would result in moderate constraints on US diesel exports and a drawdown of European stocks. Finally, an adverse scenario 
(a 35% rise in the crack spread in Q4 2026) would imply severe restrictions on US diesel exports and a delayed European response.

What impact will this have on inflation in the euro area? 
According to our estimates, the rise in diesel prices would exacerbate the expected peak in inflation in the euro area without altering 
its timing. In our baseline scenario for September 2026 (see our recently published EcoPerspective), headline inflation would peak 
at 4.1% y/y in January 2027 before gradually falling back towards 2% by the end of the year. The two least adverse scenarios 
would add only a limited amount to the peak: +0.1 pp in the first scenario (+5% for the crack spread in Q4 compared with the end 
of Q3) and +0.2 pp in the intermediate scenario (+10%), with inflation reaching 4.2% and 4.3% respectively in January 2027. 
However, there is an upside risk in the second scenario linked to the uncertain extent of the spillov er effects. In both cases, the 
main impact would be felt through fuel prices (which quickly reflect rising market prices) and would fade over the course of 2027. 
In the case of the adverse scenario (+35%), the additional inflation would be close to 0.6 pp by early 2027, pushing inflation above 
4.6%. A shock of this magnitude would, in fact, reduce businesses’ ability to absorb rising costs, which would lead to price increases 
across a broader range of goods (food, industrial goods). Subsequently, the effect would only fade gradually.

Pascal Devaux 
Economist, Middle East, Western Balkans – Energy 
pascal.devaux@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe, Eurozone, International Trade 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

3 Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp, Europe’s main hub for the storage of raw materials.

Document 403: US MARKET CALL_ Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail

Source PDF: all report pdf/US MARKET CALL_ Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail
Date:    3 hours ago
US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is
Too Big To Fail
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 17 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Nils Rasmusson / Unsplash
I. Bonds
We've all been suffering from AI fatigue for a while. The daily news about it
has been head-spinning. We've been warned that AI is a bubble that will
burst, just as the late-1990s tech bubble led to the Tech Wreck in the early
2000s. Analogies to the credit excesses of the Great Financial Crisis have

### Page 3

also been proffered. Last week's rout in AI-related stocks convinced the
naysayers that the AI bubble is bursting, just as they’ve long predicted it
would.
Our happier spin is that the Bond Vigilantes are now doing a good job of
regulating and allocating capital to the AI ecosystem. Credit-quality
spreads are widening to reflect the riskiness of the bonds issued by some
of the dodgier AI-related borrowers like CoreWeave and Oracle. The
rapidly rising cost of credit for such borrowers should deflate the AI bubble

without bursting it. If some AI-related credits do blow up, the Godfathers of
the AI mafia (along with their protectors in the government) will clean up
the mess. As in "The Godfather II," Michael Corleone has too much at
stake to let his brother Fredo screw up the family business.
Meanwhile, the rout in the AI corporate bond market has triggered a flight
to quality and safety that has stopped the rout in the US Treasury bond
market (chart). This confirms our thesis that the recent rise in US bond

yields mostly reflects better-than-expected economic growth. Tougher
credit conditions in the AI corporate bond market should curb some of the
AI excesses. However, we doubt this will trigger an economy-wide credit
crunch or a recession. We aren't convinced that it will slow capital
spending much, since labor and materials shortages, along with public
NIMBY pushback, have already slowed the pace of data center
construction.
II. S&P Earnings

### Page 4

The big banks reported mixed but solid overall Q3 earnings last week. On
average, they met banking industry analysts' expectations. Last week, the
consensus EPS for all analysts covering S&P 500 companies edged up to
$365.70 this year, $424.19 next year, and $492.62 in 2028 (chart). Those
are extremely bullish expectations considering that we are still forecasting
$500 in 2030! If the analysts are right, the S&P 500 could rise to 10,000
by the end of next year, assuming a forward P/E of 20.0-21.0. For now, we

are sticking with 10,000 by the end of the Roaring 2020s, i.e., at the end
of 2029.
Last week, Q3's consensus EPS growth rate remained at 24.0% y/y, while
the Q4 estimate rose to 29.8% (chart). We've been among the most
bullish investment strategists on the Street, but analysts are still ahead of
us.

### Page 5

The S&P 500, S&P 400, and S&P 600 continued to rise to record highs
last week (chart).
III. Stock Valuation
The recent stock market selloff has been driven by falling forward P/Es
even as forward earnings have been rising to record highs (chart). The
former are nearing the troughs they made during last year's "Liberation
Day" lows.
Since roughly mid-2025, rising FEMO (fabulous earnings momentum) has
more than offset falling FOMO (fear of missing out) (chart). Since October

6, the reverse has been true: Falling FOMO is outpacing the rise in
FEMO.

### Page 6

IV. Performance
The stock market had a tough week last week. Leading the 1.95%
pullback was MAGS (down 3.89%), while XMAGS fell 1.38% (chart). The
Russell 2000 fell 2.4%, while the Nasdaq fell 4.5%. The DJIA rose 0.67%
for the week. The Impressive 493 is still outperforming the Magnificent-7
on a year-to-date basis, with a gain of 14.1% versus 10.2%.
V. Sentiment
The market letter writers have been more bullish than retail investors
lately (chart). Odds are they will both be a bit more pessimistic this week.

### Page 7

Only 47.4% of the S&P 500 are trading above their 200-day moving
averages (chart).
VI. Commodities
Aluminum prices are falling after matching their 2022 peak. This suggests
that supply-chain disruptions attributable to Gulf War III are receding, as
they had done after Russia invaded Ukraine (chart).

### Page 8

Copper prices remained near their recent record high last week despite
chatter that tightening credit conditions could slow the AI buildout and
weigh on overall GDP growth (chart).
The outlook for higher-for-longer diesel prices offers no relief (chart).

### Page 9

We aren't giving up on gold yet. The current correction resembles
previous ones that occurred in the middle of significant gold bull runs
(chart). This time, rising interest rates have been weighing on the metal's
price. However, significant buying by the People's Bank of China and
other central banks isn't interest-rate sensitive. We are still aiming for a
gold price of $5,000 an ounce by the end of this year or early next year.

MAIN POINTS:
1. The core PCE index increased +0.25% in August, somewhat below expectations, and
the year-over-year rate increased 3.01%. We estimate that the Dallas Fed’s trimmed
mean PCE measure increased 0.16% month over month and 2.20% year over year.
2. Year-over-year core PCE was revised downward 0.36pp in July to 2.98%, primarily
reflecting the methodological revisions to the calculation of the portfolio management
(-0.23pp), computer accessories and software (-0.11pp), and legal services (+0.01pp)

components of PCE. These downward revisions were larger than we expected, mostly
reflecting a larger revision to the portfolio management component than we expected.
3. Today’s inflation report, especially the downward revisions resulting from the
methodological changes, imply that core PCE inflation is likely to substantially
undershoot the median FOMC participant’s forecast of 3.4% for 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis.
We now expect a 3.0% increase for core PCE in 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis. Coupled with

comments from New York Fed President John Williams yesterday that “with the policy
action we took at our September meeting, there is no need for urgency, and we have
time to gather more information,” we now think that an October hike is unlikely.  We are
pushing back the second hike in our forecast to December, and we see a strong chance
that the FOMC will ultimately conclude that additional rate hikes are unnecessary.
4. Real GDP was revised up by 0.7pp to +2.2% in Q2 (quarter-over-quarter annualized),

largely reflecting upward revisions to consumption (+0.4pp to +3.8%) and investment
(+1.9pp to +4.6%) growth. The strength in consumer spending in Q2 likely continued to
reflect the boost to consumer cashflow from larger-than-usual tax refunds, but we
continue to expect consumer spending growth to slow somewhat in 2026Q4 as the fiscal
support fades and the headwind from higher energy prices remains. Stronger
investment growth reflected an upward revision to private fixed investment (+0.5pp to

+9.0%), driven by data center investment in the nonresidential structures category
(+1.9pp to +0.1%), and an upward revision to the contribution from inventories (+0.2pp
to -0.5pp).
5. Reflecting today’s annual revisions to the national accounts, real GDP was revised up
by 0.4pp to +2.5% in Q1 (quarter-over-quarter annualized), largely reflecting upward
revisions to the contributions from consumption and exports. Revisions for prior years
were modest: real GDP growth on a Q4/Q4 basis was unrevised for 2025 (+2.0%) and

2023 (+3.4%), and slightly revised down for 2024 (-0.2pp to +2.4%) and up for 2022
30 September 2026   2
Goldman Sachs

### Page 3

(+0.1pp to +1.3%). 
6. Personal income increased 0.2% in August, below expectations. Year-over-year 
disposable personal income growth reached 4.8% in August. Personal spending rose 
0.9% in August, in line with consensus expectations. The savings rate decreased 0.5pp to 
4.1% in August, after a large 1.6pp upward revision to the savings rate last month driven 
by an upward revision to the level of disposable personal income (+1.2pp contribution) 
and a downward revision to personal spending as a share of DPI (+0.4pp). 
7. According to the Advance Economic Indicators Report, the goods trade deficit 
widened by $13.7bn to $132.6bn (seasonally adjusted) in August, well above 
expectations. Goods exports increased by $3.7bn to $203.4bn, largely driven by an 
increase in industrial supplies exports. Goods imports increased by $17.4bn to 
$336.1bn, driven by a large increase in industrial supplies and capital goods imports. 
8. According to the ADP report, private sector employment rose by 90k in September 
(month-over-month, seasonally adjusted), slightly above consensus expectations. 
Employment in the services sector rose 59k, primarily reflecting a 55k increase in 
education and health services and a 22k increase in leisure and hospitality employment 
that were partly offset by a 16k decrease in financial activities employment. 
Employment in goods-producing industries increased by 31k, driven by a 17k increase in 
manufacturing and a 15k increase in construction employment. ADP’s new median 
annual base pay measure, which better tracks other measures of wage growth than its 
gross pay measure, increased 3.0% for job stayers, unchanged from the August pace, 
and 4.8% for job changers, 0.1pp above the August pace. We left our forecast for August 
nonfarm payroll growth unchanged at +80k (vs. +90k consensus) ahead of Friday’s 
release. 
9. The details of the personal income and spending report were firmer than our previous 
GDP tracking assumptions. While the trade-related details of the Advance Economic 
Indicators report were much weaker than our previous assumptions, suggesting notably 
stronger imports growth in Q3 than we previously assumed, the inventories details were 
significantly stronger. On net, we lowered our Q3 GDP tracking estimate by 0.1pp to 
+3.3% (quarter-over-quarter annualized).

Document 414: What sector analysis tells us about Europe's recovery, two years after the _Draghi_ report (2026-10-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/What sector analysis tells us about Europe's recovery, two years after the _Draghi_ report (2026-10-12).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026EDITORIAL
WHAT SECTOR ANALYSIS TELLS US ABOUT EUROPE'S RECOVERY, TWO YEARS AFTER THE "DRAGHI" REPORT
1 That amounts to EUR 800 billion to be committed in the EU by the end of the decade, which would bring defence budgets to 3.5% of GDP , up from 2.4% of GDP in
2026 (estimated figure). See Colliac, S. (2026), “Readiness 2030: One Year After Its Launch, the European Rearmament Plan Is on Track,” BNP Paribas EcoWeek, March.

2 Since 2025, critical metals have also been the focus of “strategic projects” for extraction in Europe, with the European Commission having just launched an ambi-
tious second wave, targeting lithium and cobalt in particular. See: Commission Selects 46 New Strategic Projects to Bolster the EU’s Supply of Critical Raw Materials
3 See Derrien G. (2026), “Next Generation EU: The Program Is Nearing Its End, but Its Impact on Economic Activity Will Endure,” BNP Paribas Chart of the Week, Sep-

tember 23.
4 NextGenerationEU Shows Strong Delivery Record as Implementation Reaches the Finish Line, October 7, 2026.
In 2026, the European economy will not perform better than it did in 2025. At 1.4% (IMF estimate), its growth remains half
as fast as in the United States. Still, there are more and more signs that Europe is regaining strength. The energy crisis,
which has now been ongoing for eight months, would have caused it to halt in other times. Beneath an apparent sluggish

activity, productivity is rebounding, while many sectors are picking up steam.
Insufficient growth and innovation, a technological lag, and an ex -
cessive dependence on China and the United States: two years ago,
the “Draghi” report painted a troubling picture of Europe and laid the
groundwork for a massive effort to revive the economy. Almost im -
mediately afterwards, the European Union (EU) adopted a “competi -
tiveness compass,” with the primary aim of strengthening its strategic

autonomy.
A 3D OUTLOOK: DEFENCE, DIGITALISATION, DECARBONISATION
Unsurprisingly given the geopolitical context, the most significant ini -
tiatives focused on defence: since March 2025, the SAFE (Security Ac -
tion for Europe) instrument has enabled the Commission to raise funds
on the markets (up to EUR 150 billion) to support member states in
implementing the “Readiness 2030” rearmament programme 1. Here,
the planning stage has long been surpassed, and disbursements for

the acquisition of military equipment are accelerating. According to the
EDA (European Defense Agency), European defence spending is projec-
ted to reach EUR 454 billion in 2026, already 30% more than in 2024.
The EU-27 have also set out to catch up in the field of artificial intel -
ligence (AI), committing up to EUR 200 billion in investments through
public-private partnerships. From fostering a culture of AI to promoting
and retaining talents, supporting startups, and sharing experiences and

data, the initiative is designed to be comprehensive. Its cornerstone is a
dedicated EUR 20 billion fund, co-financed by the Commission and the
European Investment Bank, which aims to establish five “giga-facto -
ries” across Europe capable of hosting and training the most powerful
AI models.
The EU is also leading the counteroffensive on other fronts, such as the
supply of critical metals, which it is seeking to secure through trade
agreements (most recently with Australia and South Africa) 2, and the

accelerated decarbonisation of the economy (with specific support pro-
vided to energy-intensive sectors). Its “Clean Industry Pact” is expected
to receive up to EUR 100 billion in funding from the Innovation Fund or
the European Union Emissions Trading System.
BENEATH THE SURFACE WINDS, SUPPORTIVE CURRENTS
In a report dated June 2026, the Institut Montaigne estimated that
nearly one-third of the recommendations in the “Draghi” report had
been implemented. Even before taking up the report, the EU had

already launched a significant investment effort through its NextGe -
nerationEU (NGEU) program: EUR 573 billion distributed via the
Recovery and Resilience Facility (RRF) between 2020 and 2026, with a
focus on energy infrastructure (particularly renewables), railway, di -
gital & connectivity 3. According to the Commission, two-thirds of the
6,000 “milestones and targets” assigned to governments have already
been linked to the RRF4.

### Page 2
